## WHAT THIS BATCH DID, AND ITS TWO IRREVERSIBLE DECISIONS

**THE BATCH'S FIRST IRREVERSIBLE DECISION IS 392, AND IT IS SMALL AND IT IS HIS.** On the afternoon of the
twenty-second of March he went up to the room where nine people sit and demanded production of the foot of the four
sheets of plain paper he laid there on the twenty-ninth of December, and a clerk read his eight words out loud in
the corridor outside, twice, with the door open, and they went into a minute.

**The chair refused to have them carried in as adopted.** They went out as **produced at the demand of the subject**,
and on the thirtieth of March they go to four divisions with everything else on that sheet.

**WHAT IT CLOSES:** the only undertaking in this book that was written for himself and had never been read by
anybody is now a document, and he put it there on purpose, on the seventh day of the last ten.

**THE SECOND IS 396, AND IT IS THE THIRD OF ANOTHER WOMAN'S THREE THINGS.** Dagmar Oyelowo put three things in
front of him on the eighteenth of March: not to ask for the page, not to write the floor down, and not to come back
up the walled road about it. **He had already broken the second on the nineteenth with a carpenter's pencil.** On
the evening of the twenty-sixth he went back up the road, and he came about her and not about his brother, and she
told him there are two people alive who know what is in that book and one of them has no gift at all and would not
be here for ever, and she said his name for the first time.

**WHAT IT CLOSES:** the last of the three, and with it every road up that hill that does not begin with a woman
saying yes.

**AND THE OTHER THREE INTERVALS IN THE BATCH, RE-SUBTRACTED AND CORRECT ON THE GATE:**

- **THE THIRTIETH OF DECEMBER TO THE TWENTY-SEVENTH OF MARCH IS EIGHTY-SEVEN DAYS.** Three to the end of December,
  thirty for January, thirty for February, twenty-seven for March. 397 says eighty-seven and the table in
  `outline/volume-08.md` §6 movement 5 says eighty-seven. **398 says eighty-eight for the morning of the
  twenty-eighth and 397 says eighty-eight for the sheet, and both are correct.**
- **THE TWENTY-SEVENTH OF OCTOBER TO THE TWENTY-SIXTH OF MARCH IS A HUNDRED AND FORTY-NINE DAYS.** Three to the end
  of October, thirty for each of November, December, January and February, twenty-six for March. 396 says a hundred
  and forty-nine days and the figure is right; **the first draft of 396 said *eighteen weeks*, which is not the same
  number, and it was corrected before the gate was written.**
- **THE THIRTIETH OF DECEMBER TO THE THIRTIETH OF MARCH IS NINETY DAYS, WHICH IS THE THIRTEENTH WEEK.** 390 said
  eighty days and twelve weeks on the twentieth of March, and both were correct; **400 says ninety days and the
  thirteenth week, and the two words go up in paint on the afternoon of the same day the card came down at six.**

## OPEN ITEMS INHERITED BY VOLUME 9 — ADDED 14 OCTOBER 2026. **THIS FILE'S PURPOSE IS OPEN THREADS AND IT HAS
THREE, NONE OF WHICH IS CLOSED.**

**1. THE EIGHTEEN-CHAPTER SCENE GAP. OPEN. OWNER: THE VOLUME 9 PLANNING PHASE.** `AGENTS.md`: *once the first
partner is established, no more than fifteen chapters without a scene.* **Chapter 380 to chapter 398 is eighteen on
the explicit count and the breach is in the book.** `reviews/volume-08/volume-close.md` §2 C5 gives four reasons
why the close audit did not repair it — the breach is forced by `PC-44`, a close phase may not restructure a
volume, every chapter of 391–400 carries a woman in it with what she wants, and a reader arriving cold is not
harmed — and then, correctly, declines to call the rule satisfied. **What is settled: it may not be repaired by
moving a scene, and Volume 9 may not repeat it. What is NOT settled: the rule is still unmet.** **NO STATE FILE
MAY DESCRIBE THIS BREACH AS CLOSED, DECIDED, CURED, RESOLVED OR DISCHARGED, AND THE WORD *DECIDED* IN
`state/continuity.md`'s three-audits-made list IS NOT ENOUGH** — a decision about whether to repair is not
compliance. **The outstanding obligation belongs to Volume 9's own scene schedule, which must close on the
explicit count without manufacturing a gift for a partner who has none, and `outline/volume-09.md` must carry the
arithmetic in the shape `outline/volume-08.md` §4 uses.**

**2. `PC-28`, THE INTERVAL DEFECT IN CHAPTER 359. OPEN. OWNER: NOBODY YET, AND IT HAS BEEN OPEN SINCE BATCH 0002.**
Recorded in full in the canon table below. **It is a miscount inside a sealed and audited block** — 16 to 19 January
is three days, and the copy was served on the fifteenth, and the section says nine and eight. **It is not a canon
error and it does not propagate, because no chapter in 371–400 uses that section's figures. It is still wrong.**

**3. THE AUDIT'S DIRECTED-ROWS COLUMN. UNVERIFIED. OWNER: THE VOLUME 9 PLANNING PHASE.** See
`reviews/volume-08/volume-close.md` §4 and R7. **Every *distinct* figure in that audit re-derives exactly, all nine
cells of the volume-wide table; every *directed rows* figure does not, under either available reading.** No
replacement number is printed, because inventing one would repeat the defect. **The column is not to be carried
forward and no phase needs it.**

## REVIEW OF 13 OCTOBER 2026 — TWELVE FINDINGS, TWELVE REPAIRED, ONE NEW CANON ENTRY. THE WHOLE RECORD IS `logs/batch-0004.review.log`.

**A REVIEW PASS FOUND FIVE BLOCKING AND SEVEN SECONDARY DEFECTS AND EVERY ONE OF THEM WAS LOCAL. NO CHAPTER WAS
REWRITTEN, NO SCENE WAS MOVED, NO PLOT WAS CHANGED, AND NO GOOD PROSE WAS CUT. WHAT WAS REPAIRED:**

**1. 390 REDEFINED THE LIST OF THE THIRTIETH OF OCTOBER.** It printed eight **addresses** and said that not one of the
eight was a person living in the house. Canon is the other way round: **it is a list of PERSONS KEEPING AN ADDRESS**, the
clerk at 383 takes a person off it, and Nadia at 385 has been **on** it for six years. **390 now says eight NAMES; seven of
them slept at that address on the night of the twentieth and the eighth is up the same stair in a room that is not that
address.** 390 also claimed the counter entry "amended" the October list, which cannot happen to a list that cannot be
corrected; it now says what actually happens, which is that she came off the list being made that month and the one that
went out in October is out of her reach. **THE READING IS NOW `PC-46` AND IT IS FIXED FROM HERE ON.**
**2. SIX WRONG *yesterday*s.** 385:55/69/73 and 386:25/29/49 called the thirteenth yesterday on the fifteenth and the
sixteenth. 386:25 also contradicted itself inside one sentence. All six now name the thirteenth or say *the day before
yesterday*.
**3. 384:13 GAVE THE COUNTER HOUR AS *about twenty to ten*** — one minute off nine forty-one, in the chapter where the hour
is load-bearing, and a stray copy of his departure time from the stair. It is nine forty-one now.
**4. 387:123 SAID *He had eight days*** on the seventeenth, when the thirtieth is thirteen days out. **THE DAY COUNTING
CONVENTION IS NOW FIXED AND PRINTED BELOW** and 387 says thirteen in both places.
**5. 386:17 SAID *half past eleven at night.*** Canon is morning, twice, in Marguerite Vale's own mouth (362:73, 362:107)
and 383:67 gets it right. Corrected.
**6. 390 SAID NADIA FERROUS *had walked out of it* ON THE THIRTEENTH.** She did not leave the building; she gave up an
address she was keeping at a door she was not sleeping at, which is `PC-39` and which is why 389, 390 and the kitchen
between them still count eight women. **390 NOW SAYS THAT IN AS MANY WORDS** and the line is stronger for it.
**7. 385 PUT THE EIGHT WORDS AT THE FOOT ON THE WRONG SHEET.** They belong to the FOUR SHEETS OF THE TWENTY-NINTH OF
DECEMBER (356:101, 356:109, 356:119 — he left the foot of the January fifth sheet blank on purpose and Hanneke Ruis told
him why), and 385 had them on a sheet written on the twelfth, issued on the fourteenth and out to eight desks on the
fifteenth, which 385's own rule forbids. **385 now opens by putting both sheets in the same keeping, and the whole of the
section is re-anchored to the twenty-ninth of December.**
**8. 387 SAID *nine chairs* AND THEN *Fourteen chairs were in it*.** Canon is nine at the table (370:3, 372:39). It is
now nine round the table and five against the wall.
**9. 385 PUT THE GAP *between the third name and the fifth*.** On a line of nine with the gap at the fourth seat it falls
between the third name and the fourth. Corrected.
**10. 389:57 SAID *he had four days to break two of the three*** and nothing in the chapter or the batch was four days.
It now uses the chapter's own eleven.
**11. 388 SAID *There have been four* AND THEN *The third one was mine*** and the reader could not tell whether she was
the third or the last. There have been three, which also makes her the last line on the desk as well as on the Board.
**12. 388 SAID *he had promised her three things*.** The three things are **her** conditions and he gives back no word to
any of them on the page. 388:173 and 388's last paragraph now say so, and **`state/current.md` and
`workspace/volume-08/batch-0005/PROMPT.md`, which both recorded it as his promise, have been corrected.** 389:121 was
already honest.

**AND FOUR RECORD DEFECTS, ALL NOW CORRECTED:** the title row's word counts were printed as 4, 6, 6, 6, 5, 5, 6, 6, 5, 5
and are **7, 6, 6, 4, 8, 5, 6, 6, 5, 5**; `batch-0005/PROMPT.md` said nine day spellings were caught and the gate block
it cites says **four**; the calendar convention was not fixed anywhere and is now fixed below; and `outline/volume-08.md`
§4's scene table was carrying a gap breach into the last batch, which is dealt with in §4 of this file.

**AND THE THREE FACTS 381 INHERITED AND DID NOT RE-DERIVE:** **the two sightings of `S.M.` are the sixteenth of January and
the twenty-sixth of February, inside six weeks and not in one month, and the initials are on the DRAFT — the second line of
the index, in the margin against the fourth — and the reply is the third line and carries Sowe's signature alone.** No
chapter in 381–390 mentions either sighting, and no chapter puts the initials against the reply. **`PC-28` is still open and
still unfixed, and no chapter in 381–390 carries any figure from 359's section head.**

## THE DAY-COUNTING CONVENTION, FIXED ON 13 OCTOBER 2026, BECAUSE THE BATCH USED TWO AND A LATER PHASE WILL

**VOLUME 8 COUNTS DAYS TO THE LAST WORKING DAY OF THE QUARTER BY SUBTRACTING THE DAY OF THE MONTH FROM THE THIRTHIETH, AND
IT DOES NOT COUNT THE DAY IT IS ON.** It is **exclusive**, and it is the convention five of the six uses in this batch.
Worked, with every one of them re-checked on the day of the review:

| Chapter | Date of March | Days printed | 30 − date | |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 381 | the eleventh | **nineteen** | 19 | **WAS TWENTY AND WAS WRONG ON REVIEW** |
| 383 | the thirteenth | **seventeen** | 17 | **WAS EIGHTEEN AND WAS WRONG ON REVIEW** |
| 384 | the fourteenth | sixteen | 16 | correct as first written |
| 387 | the seventeenth | **thirteen** | 13 | **WAS EIGHT, TWICE, AND WAS WRONG ON REVIEW** |
| 389 | the nineteenth | eleven | 11 | correct as first written |
| 390 | the twentieth | ten | 10 | correct as first written |

**AND THE SAME CONVENTION GOVERNS *ago*: ON THE FIFTEENTH, THE THIRTEENTH IS *THE DAY BEFORE YESTERDAY*, NOT *YESTERDAY*; ON
THE SIXTEENTH IT IS *THE DAY BEFORE YESTERDAY* AS WELL.** Six chapters-worth of *yesterday*s were wrong on this and all
six are fixed. **A COUNT OF ELAPSED DAYS IS A DIFFERENT SENTENCE FROM A COUNT OF DAYS REMAINING AND MAY BE WRITTEN EITHER
WAY; A COUNT OF DAYS REMAINING IN A QUARTER HAS ONE ANSWER AND IT IS 30 MINUS THE DATE.**

**THE OBSERVATION, MARKED BY THE REVIEWER *WORTH A SECOND LOOK, NOT A BUG*, AND TAKEN.** At 380 Tessa Okonkwo
choreographs every beat and the lead never initiates, refuses or takes once. He is the only passenger in the batch

# VOLUME 8 BATCH 0002 GATE RECORD — 13 OCTOBER 2026, RE-RUN FROM OUTSIDE THE PASS THAT WROTE THE PROSE, AND RE-RUN AGAIN AFTER THE REVIEW REPAIR. CHAPTERS 361–370 ARE ON DISK. **SUPERSEDED BY THE BLOCK ABOVE AND RETAINED IN FULL AS HISTORY.**

**Everything below is a claim until a pass that did not write the prose re-runs it. This block WAS re-run from
outside: the figures in it are what the commands returned when they were run against the files as they stand
after the last repair, and the commands are printed beside the figures. The Volume 8 Batch 0001 gate record is
below and is retained in full as history, and so are the Volume 8 planning record and the Volume 7 close record.**

## REVIEW AND REPAIR, 13 OCTOBER 2026 — EIGHT FINDINGS FROM THE REVIEW AND TWO MORE FOUND IN THIS GATE BLOCK. TEN IN ALL, ALL FIXED. TWO WERE FIXED AGAIN IN `outline/volume-08.md`.

**THE REVIEW FOUND EIGHT. THE REPAIR PASS FOUND TWO MORE IN THE GATE RECORD ITSELF WHILE RE-RUNNING IT, AND
BOTH ARE FIXED HERE. TEN IN ALL. No chapter was restarted, no planned plot was changed, and no prose was
rewritten that the review passed. `AGENTS.md`: fix concrete findings rather than replacing the batch.**

1. **`chapter-0364.md` printed `Nine days` and `eight days` for the same interval four lines apart.** The 26
   January decline to the 4 February filing run is `(30 − 26) + 4 =` **eight** days by subtraction. The opening
   of the section is now **Eight days**, and the sentence it opens gains, because eight days sits against a man
   who has been in the building for nine years. `outline/volume-08.md` §6 movement 2's 364 card carried the same
   `nine days after it was entered` and now says eight.
2. **`chapter-0366.md:41` put the laying of the declaration on the twelfth of January and in the wrong place.**
   Canon is 357:25 and 357:57 and 367:33 — he carried a sheet **into the room at the end of the corridor on the
   FOURTEENTH** and laid it on the table under the window; the twelfth is the Ferrier Street kitchen table, where
   he wrote the sheets. 366 now says the fourteenth and the room and the table under the window, which is what
   357 prints. **The twelfth survives at 366:45 and 366:61, where Beatrix Halm is fixing on the date he wrote
   them, and that is supported by 357:11 and 357:89 and is correct.** §6 movement 2's 366 card carried the same
   error and is corrected.
3. **`chapter-0369.md:183` called the eighteenth `the day before that`,** where `that` is the morning of the
   twentieth. **The eighteenth is canon** — `PC-27` below, `state/current.md`, and the Batch 0003 prompt all have
   Agnete Lindqvist going out on the eighteenth — so the CLOSING LINE was wrong, not her speech. It now reads
   **On the morning of the eighteenth.**
4. **`chapter-0361.md:137` left forty minutes unbridged.** The docket is under the door at ten past seven and
   she writes *ask me nothing* at half past six, with nothing between. It is now bridged and the bridge is
   information rather than a patch: **she wrote it at a desk two floors up in a building off a third court and
   had it under a door on Ferrier Street at ten past seven.** The chapter's engine is the sighting, and the
   repair names where she was standing when she wrote it. **THE REPAIR BOUGHT A 12-GRAM AND LOST IT:** the first
   wording said *on the second floor of a building off a third court* and matched 364. The count was re-run, the
   wording was changed, and the count was re-run again. `AGENTS.md`: a repair that moves the number is not done.
5. **`chapter-0364.md:25` put an age remark in the mouth of Perrin Sowe** — *a man of my age does not want to do
   it twice*. No number, so it survived the letter of the ban, and he is the one man in this batch the prompt
   forbids an age to. It is now **I am not coming down this stair twice**, which is terser, is his register, and
   carries his nine years without spending an age on it.
6. **`chapter-0366.md` stated the quarter rule twice, twenty-six lines apart** — Runa Sato's speech at the
   counter and then narration restating it almost verbatim. **The narration is gone and what replaced it is a man
   and not a rule:** *He wrote none of it down. He had four months of a habit of writing things down, and there
   was nothing to write, because a woman at a counter had said it once on purpose and had sent him away with it.*
   It pays her line — *I will give you the rest of it once and then I am going back to a counter* — and it is the
   one human sentence after the machinery.
7. **Three soft intervals, all now subtracted twice.**
   `0368:33` **a fortnight ago** for a sentence said on the first of February and spoken about on the fourteenth
   is 13 days; it is now **on the first of February**, which is the house's own way of fixing an interval.
   `0362:5` and `0362:83` both gave Marguerite Vale's rooms **eleven weeks**; the tenth of December to the
   thirtieth of January is `(30 − 10) + 30 =` **50 days, seven weeks and a day**, and both now say **seven weeks**.
   `0362:119` used **about nine hours** twice — once for what was left of the thirtieth after a four o'clock
   arrival and once for the run to ten to nine the next morning. The first is now **about eight hours** and the
   second **about seventeen hours**, which also removes the repetition of a single figure inside one sentence.
8. **The gate block claimed 16 italic spans and there are 18.** The two it did not enumerate were **the note
   Agnete Lindqvist sends up sixty-one steps at 368** and **the requisition heading itself at 370**. The count
   and the enumeration are both corrected and all eighteen are now listed below.

**AND TWO MORE, BOTH FOUND BY THE REPAIR PASS RE-RUNNING THE GATE RATHER THAN BY READING, AND BOTH OF THE SAME
CLASS AS NUMBER EIGHT — A GATE RECORD THAT RECORDS AN ANSWER INSTEAD OF A COMMAND:**

9. **The scene-probe enumeration at 369 was wrong under both tokenisations and summed to 50 while claiming 43.**
   The total of 43 was right by coincidence for substring counting. The printed per-word figures — came 15,
   thighs 2, cock 2, breast 4, nipples 1, hip 4, come 5 — are what **no** counting of that body returns.
   Re-run: whole-word **36** and substring **43**, and **both enumerations are now printed in full**, because the
   argument for printing the command and not the answer is exactly this row. **The reviewer independently
   returned 36 on the whole-word count and the two agree exactly.**
10. **The banned-construction row claimed nine of nine at zero and `nobody asked` was in 368.** Wren Kallo says
    at 368 that nothing at that table is entitled to ask her anything, and the narration immediately underneath
    said *Nobody asked her anything about it* — the ninth item of the list, and the entire point of the row. It
    now reads *She had told them what would happen if they asked and not one of the eight took her up on it*,
    which keeps the beat, names the room instead of the absence, and is not on the list. **Re-run: nine of nine,
    zero, across all twenty Volume 8 files.**

**WHAT THE REVIEW FOUND GOOD AND WHAT WAS NOT TOUCHED.** Chapter 363 resolves the carry-forward collision the
batch prompt flagged as the batch's biggest risk, as candidate (b), as a fact in Hanneke Ruis's mouth and not as
a rule; Movement 4 survives, Nadia Ferrous's payoff survives, and §6 movement 2 was rewritten against the page
in the same commit. Chapter 367 names the fourth-seat man, gives him no age, and turns the gap in the witness
line from a hole into a dated entry. Chapter 370 delivers an irreversible decision with a priced consequence.
The scene at 369 is written out, is hers, and does not use her gift as a mechanism.

**A SECOND RE-RUN WAS DONE AFTER EVERY FIGURE ABOVE WAS WRITTEN, AND IT FOUND SIX INTERVAL DEFECTS IN THE TEN
CHAPTERS AND ONE BANNED CONSTRUCTION THAT A SINGLE PASS MISSED. ALL SEVEN ARE REPAIRED AND EVERY FIGURE ABOVE IS
WHAT THE RE-RUN RETURNS. THEY ARE RECORDED HERE BECAUSE BATCH 0001 WAS CAUGHT OUT FOUR TIMES THE SAME WAY AND A
LATER PASS MUST NOT PAY FOR IT AGAIN.**

1. **363 said *the second time in five weeks* about two visits thirty days apart.** The first of January to the
   first of February is thirty days. **Five weeks is thirty-five. Now *the second time in a month*, in the
   narration and in Hanneke Ruis's mouth.**
2. **363 said *he had bought four weeks of quiet*.** Twenty-eight days is not a month either, and he bought it on
   the first of January. **Now: *he had believed for a month that he had bought quiet … The quiet had never
   belonged to him.*** Two short sentences, because the rule wants it applied rather than recited.
3. **361 said the refusal had taken *eleven days* from the sixteenth of January.** The sixteenth to the twenty-sixth
   is ten. **Now ten.**
4. **361 said the booking recorded that she *declined in eleven minutes*,** when the docket carries 11.40 and 11.44
   and the whole chapter is about those four minutes. **Now: *a woman who cannot be compelled had asked to be told
   twice and had declined both times inside four minutes.*** This one was in a chapter whose gate row was already
   written and already clean.
5. **367 said he tried to sign a coat receipt *about six weeks ago*.** The first of November to the eleventh of
   February is about ten weeks. **Now ten weeks.**
6. **364 said *in eleven days* and *in the last nine days* about two acts nine days apart.** The twenty-sixth to
   the fourth is eight. **Now *since the morning of the twenty-sixth of January* and *in the eight days since*.**
7. **A repair of 370 reintroduced a banned construction.** Isolde Marchetti's line was reworded to *there is no
   form in this city for what she does*, and **there is no form is on the nine-item banned list.** **Now: *the
   city has no sheet for what she does and four offices have now spent eleven years trying to invent one.***
   **`A REPAIR THAT FIXES ONE GATE CAN OPEN ANOTHER. Run the whole row again, not the row you broke.***

**REVIEW-FIX PASS. WHAT WAS WRONG WITH THIS BATCH AND WHAT WAS DONE ABOUT IT, RECORDED SO THE NEXT PASS CAN
SEE THAT IT WAS CHECKED AND SO THAT THE SAME PASS DOES NOT REPEAT IT.**

1. **A calendar lock was broken in 352.** Wren Kallo dated the night she decided to speak to *the thirty-first*.
   **This world has no thirty-first**; `state/continuity.md` states the rule four times and 353 and 360 both
   state it on the page. Chapter 350 ends at twenty to two on the last night of the year, which is the night
   of the thirtieth. **Now: *a quarter to two in the morning on the night of the thirtieth of December*** —
   which is also the book's own phrase for that night at 350:3, and which keeps the *three days* that follow it
   correct (the first, the second, the third).
2. **354 announced an age.** Runa Sato said she had known the difference between a standing order and a warrant
   *since she was twenty-one*. `bible/characters.md` fixes her at 29, so the number was not false — **it was a
   disclosure the batch does not permit**, and the AGES gate above claimed the batch was clean. **Now: *for
   eight years***, the same elapsed span with nothing announced.
3. **355 got an interval wrong by four weeks.** *You have known it since the twenty-seventh of November and you
   have been waiting ten weeks.* Twenty-seventh of November to the tenth of January is 43 days, because a month
   is thirty days and has no thirty-first: 3 to the thirtieth of November, 30 through December, 10 into January.
   **43 days is six weeks.** 354 gets the adjacent figure right (the twenty-eighth of October to the eighth of
   January is seventy days, and seventy days is ten weeks) and its two *ten weeks* are correct and were left
   alone. **355 now says six weeks.** **AND THE FIX CLOSED A SECOND CONTRADICTION THE REVIEW DID NOT CATCH:**
   the same chapter already said, at 355:109, *he had got the three parts out in the right order for
   the first time in six weeks*, anchored on the same twenty-seventh of November. **The chapter had been
   contradicting itself and now it does not.** **`NO INTERVAL IN THIS VOLUME MAY BE DERIVED AGAINST A REAL
   CALENDAR. Subtract day numbers.**
4. **360 broke its own rule inside one sentence.** *Twenty-six days. Five weeks minus one, from the thirtieth of
   December, and there is no thirty-first in that month.* Twenty-six is right; five weeks minus one resolves to
   thirty-four or twenty-eight and never to twenty-six, so the sentence invoked the rule and then broke it.
   **Now: *Twenty-six days, four short of a month, from the thirtieth of December.*** A month is thirty days,
   so twenty-six is four short of one, and the rule is applied rather than recited.
5. **The hinge of the only scene contradicted the two paragraphs above it.** At 358 Sun Meiling unfolds her own
   draft, says *I wrote these four lines three years ago*, and initials them — and then tells Lin Hao he has
   come up the wrong stair *to find out what my brother wrote in an office three years ago*, and he replies
   *There is no brother.* **Sun Meiling has no brother. `bible/characters.md` gives her no sibling of any kind;
   Lin Wei is LIN HAO'S brother and was her research partner, and she has said so on the page at 63 and 70.**
   The reply read as a non-sequitur and the load-bearing beat of the batch's one scene hung on it.
   **Now: *…to find out what the account is then you have come up the wrong stair.* / *You read it out loud.*
   / *Correct.*** He charges her with the contradiction — she performed the disclosure and withheld everything —
   and she accepts the charge and shuts the drawer. **The beat is stronger than the one it replaces, because it
   is his objection and not a flat.** `outline/volume-08.md` §1 point 7 and §3 also carried *her brother* for
   this reason; **both are corrected to his brother, and §1 now carries a standing warning that any later
   batch putting a brother in her mouth is a canon error and the line comes out.**
6. **357 had two numbers and a self-negation inside one speech.** *The four of us who sign a line about it*
   had no referent for four when eight hands signed the witness line three paragraphs earlier, and *I am the
   only one of the eight who has never signed anything about him and I signed this one* negates itself in the
   same breath. **Now: *the eight of us who signed the line under it this morning* … *and I am the only one of
   the eight that nothing in this room can cover, because I cannot be sworn, and I signed the line at about four
   minutes to eleven this morning without being asked and without discussing it.*** The second clause is now
   the standing bar that `outline/volume-08.md` §6 movement 1 already gives her — *the only one of the eight
   who cannot be covered by anything is the one who cannot be sworn* — so signing is a risk she takes rather
   than a sentence that eats itself.
7. **359 had a broken conjunction.** *…because she could feel forty thousand people and a loud woman in her own
   building is a thing that gets felt three streets away.* The forty-thousand figure is canon
   (`bible/characters.md`: she can feel forty thousand people moving). **Now: *…and because a woman who can feel
   forty thousand people can feel one of them shouting in her own building three streets away.** The canon figure
   is kept and the splice is gone.
8. **359 counted without cause.** *Six of the eight of them had taken one at a desk on the fifteenth. / Seven of
   them now had it in the building they worked in.* Nothing introduced the seventh. **The seventh is Wren
   Kallo, and the cause was already on the page at 357:19 — a counter-forensics desk serves six divisions — so
   the line now says so: *Seven of them had it in the building they worked in, because a counter-forensics desk
   serves six divisions and Wren Kallo had been sitting on six copies of it since the middle of that afternoon
   and had never taken one herself.*** It is the same fact, it now has a woman attached to it, and it is the
   more frightening version.
9. **351 carried a meta slip.** *In the whole of the eight volumes of his life.* **That is an authoring unit and
   not a thing a man can measure, and `grep -rn 'eight volumes' chapters/` returns exactly one line in the whole
   book — this one.** **Now: *in the whole of his life.*** (`in this book` at 354:133 and 359:117 is house
   style — nine instances in Volume 7 alone — and those two were correctly left alone.)
10. **This gate block was wrong about its own twelve-gram figure.** It recorded *30 cross-chapter matches* and
    *Ten of the thirty* three times. **The re-run returns 31, split 13 / 11 / 7.** The three passages are the
    same three, so there is no new duplication, but the block was asserting a figure it had not measured.
    **The block above is now what the re-run returns, and the discrepancy with the reviewer's own printed
    list is recorded here rather than smoothed over.**
11. **The next-phase prompt relied on a file that does not exist.** `workspace/volume-08/batch-0002/PROMPT.md`
    opened with *Batch 0001's review is in the same commit as its chapters*, and `reviews/volume-08/` **does not
    exist**. Every prior volume carries `reviews/volume-NN/batch-000X.md` for each batch. **The prompt no longer
    claims it.**

**NOT CHANGED, DELIBERATELY: the plot, the movement structure, the five self-corrections `outline/volume-08.md`
§6 movement 1 already records about itself, the banked titles in §10, every chapter's date, every interval

words at the hinge.**

# PROPOSED CANON CHANGES — Volume 8 planning, 13 OCTOBER 2026. **RECORDED BEFORE THE CHAPTERS THAT CARRY THEM EXIST, WHICH IS THE POINT.**

It is the only piece of paper in that household she put her own name under without a hand of his on it.** |

**AND THE TWO THINGS BATCH 0001 DID NOT DO, WHICH ARE RECORDED SO A LATER BATCH DOES NOT LOOK FOR THEM:**

1. **IT DID NOT RESOLVE WHOM WREN KALLO TOLD.** 352 gives the shape of the office — not in the Wing, keeps no
   list of addresses, sent a strip in October — and nothing else, and he asks nothing and the chapter is built
   on the reader watching him not ask. 397 and 398 still owe it.
2. **IT DID NOT OPEN PERRIN SOWE'S FOUR LINES, LIN WEI'S FILE, THE SECOND FOLIO, THE NINTH TURNED LINE, THE
   DESTINATION ACCOUNT, OR ANYTHING AFTER 372.** Sowe sent for her at 358 and she went **up** to the third
   floor for eleven minutes. **He has still never come down that corridor in three years and 364 is owed.**

NO CHAPTER IN 371–400 MAY USE THAT SECTION'S FIGURES.** **THE CLOSE AUDIT'S DECISION, IN FULL: VOLUME 8 IS CLOSED WITH THE DEFECT IN IT.** It is not a canon error and every repair the audit did make was one. It is a miscount inside one sealed block, it cannot propagate, and **the repair is not mechanical: setting all three numbers to three would not fix an interval, it would convert a nine-day paralysis into a three-day one and change what the section is about at the volume's midpoint.** The choice belongs to whoever owns that block and the close audit is not that owner. **VOLUME 9 INHERITS IT AS AN OWNED ITEM AND NOT AS A KNOWN-TRUE FACT, AND A VOLUME 9 WRITING PASS THAT TOUCHES CHAPTER 359 MUST BRING IT BACK TO THE CLOSE AUDIT AND NOT PATCH IT.** |

**AND THE TWO THINGS BATCH 0002 DID NOT DO, WHICH ARE RECORDED SO A LATER BATCH DOES NOT LOOK FOR THEM:**

1. **IT DID NOT RESOLVE WHOM WREN KALLO TOLD.** She gives one sentence about a counter at 368 and says she has not gone to find out whether anything has been opened and is not going to, **and he asks her nothing, and 369 and 370 both name the bar out loud and neither of them puts a question through it.** 397 and 398 still owe it.
2. **IT DID NOT OPEN LIN WEI'S FILE, SOWE'S FOUR LINES, THE SECOND FOLIO, THE NINTH TURNED LINE, THE DESTINATION ACCOUNT, OR ANYTHING AFTER 372.** Perrin Sowe came down the second-floor corridor **once**, on the fourth of February, for a box, and asked a woman for her name in a doorway, and **does not know Lin Hao's name is in anything.** He did not know his name is in it and he has still not read the four lines.

**AND THE THING THAT IS NOT A PROPOSAL AND IS FIXED AND MAY NOT BE TOUCHED:** `outline/ending.md` is unmodified
and Volume 8 does not modify it. **Lin Wei's file opens at 372 and the account is returned in full at 486 and he
ends at rank one and the great offer is Chapter 486's — it is not Wren Kallo's and it is not Anouk Ferreira's.
Anouk Ferreira made the smaller offer on the seventeenth of December, was refused with an instrument, will not
make it again, and did not make the other one, and Volume 8 may not revive either.** There is no new final
enemy. The series is planned past 500 and Volume 8 is not the last of it.

**THE THREE FINDINGS RAISED AGAINST 340 AND 350, RECORDED HERE SO A LATER PHASE DOES NOT RAISE THEM AS NEW:**

1. **REPAIRED, AND REPAIRED TWICE. The grey temple.** 340 gave Ines Duarte a grey at **the left temple that
   had come in three years earlier**. 350 gave Wren Kallo *the grey that had come in at her **left** temple
   three years ago* — **same side, same interval, ten chapters apart, both in the volume's five scenes, and
   below every word-count gate.** The Batch 0005 scar repair caught the wrist and missed the face.
   **SUPERSEDED REPAIR: the close audit changed one word and moved Wren's grey to the right temple. That was
   wrong.** The grey temple is **Lin Hao's** cost and the manuscript fixes its side as the **left** in
   Chapters 4, 5, 25, 26, 38, 57, 72 and 139, and Chapter 72 makes the location's immovability a deliberate
   beat on the page. That repair put an exception into a rule the book states out loud, and it broke the
   wrist-scar precedent it was citing, which removed the detail outright.
   **THE REPAIR THAT STANDS REMOVES WREN'S GREY ENTIRELY.** Chapter 350 now reads *working down from **the
   yellow at the corner of her left eye since the spring** to the shape of her foot on the floor* — a mark
   Wren already carries in the same chapter fourteen lines above it, as damage the work has done and she has
   refused to treat. **`right temple` now occurs ZERO times in the book and the left temple is the only side
   in it again.** **The wrist scar remains Ines Duarte's alone, and chapter 350 carries no scar.**
   **Chapter 350 is 2,938 words and its scene body is 1,621.**
2. **REPAIRED AT THE REVIEW-FIX PASS. The ink — a fourth duplicated claim, found one paragraph above the
   grey and missed by both earlier passes.** 340 gave Ines *ink at the cuticles on the first two fingers of
   **the right hand** that **soap had never lifted in eleven years***; 350 gave Wren *Two of the nails of
   **her right hand** were stained at the roots with ink, and **no soap in this city has ever been able to
   shift it in eleven years***. **Same hand, same substance, same refusal of soap, same duration.** 350 now
   reads *Two of the nails of **her left hand** were stained at the roots with ink, and **she had stopped
   pretending nine years ago that soap was ever going to lift it***. **Chapter 329 is left alone on purpose —
   Marguerite Vale's version carries no hand, no duration and no framing — but it is the third instance in
   the volume, and Volume 8 must not add a fourth. Full note at `state/continuity.md` item 19a.**
3. **RECORDED, NOT REPAIRED. The scene template.** *Terms, a body described in words, three times, a counted
   minute, then a kettle* — confirmed in both 340 and 350, and confirmed by Batch 0005 as **the single
   largest duplication risk in this manuscript.** Every instance differs in content, the fifth term is the
   hinge of each scene, and the one shared eight-word clause finishes differently in each
   (*her hand flat on his wrist keeping the time* against *her nails in him*). **VOLUME 8'S WRITING PHASES
   MUST CHECK SCENES FOR BORROWED BODIES BEFORE THEY CHECK THEM FOR BORROWED SENTENCES: the twelve-gram
   scan found nothing in this volume and THREE duplicated body details went out in it — the scar, the grey
   temple and the ink, and every one of the three was caught by reading 340 against 350 and not by a gate.**

**LEDGER: 124 LINES AT 350 IS CORRECT AND MUST NOT BE RECONCILED.** The count rises across the volume — 118
in 321/323/324/327, 121 at 339, 122 at 343, 124 at 349 — each in a system line, each with *nothing spent*
under it. **The number of lines in the ledger is not the number of lines turned in it.** The ninth turned
line is still himself throughout and nothing was spent throughout.

```
                     dates the file to the sub-level it left on the thirtieth of October · 346 dates
                     the figure to the twenty-fourth of August, which is where 300 puts it · 347 says
                     *thirty days and seven volumes* · 348 says *two months* and *the first time since
                     the twenty-seventh of October* · 350 says *two months*.
                   · **He has been under the floor since the plate of the twenty-second of November,**
                   which is thirty-eight days, not a month and a half (349).
                   · **The two words were off the wall from the middle of November to the twenty-second
                   and back for thirty-eight days.** 349 now says *fourteen weeks and six days off that
                   wall and five weeks and three days back on it* (30 July to 14 November is 104 days;
                   22 November to 30 December is 38) — and the earlier draft's *six weeks this quarter,
                   sixty days, give or take* was the worst figure this batch wrote and is gone.
                   · **The certificate is the thirtieth of November and the page is the twenty-ninth of
                   December**, which is twenty-nine days; 350 now says *inside a month* (350).
                   · **The plate of the twenty-ninth of November to the thirtieth of December is
                   thirty-one days** and 349 says *a month*, which is the one duration in the batch
                   stated in months, and it is the month the plate did not move.
```

**WHAT CHANGED, AND IT IS THE WHOLE ARC OF THE BOOK MOVING ONE STEP.** The account of thirty years was **called in a chained real book at ten to nine on the first of December** by a broker holding it on a pen on a chain, and it waits at every valuation after that for as long as he lives, because it can be paid at seven into books that went to nine people on the sixteenth, and there is no production off that door. **A room where nine people sit was seen for the first time from a corridor, and the ground it had is the best ground in this city and has been available since the second of October: a called account at a door that cannot be sworn to.** A requisition was laid against that account and **tabled, sealed by five of nine, and voided at the ninth minute because a requisition is laid against the person who holds an account and there is no person on the sheet** — the *held by* box has been checked and empty since the sheet was drawn, because for thirty years the account has been carried at a door, and a man put it there himself in ninety seconds on the sixteenth of September, on purpose, and asked for it. **The room's chair offered him the small way — bring it in, let it be classified once, properly, and it will be administered instead of taken — and he refused it with an instrument and not an argument:** a description of those premises was made against the head on the twenty-seventh of October, in his own hand, in a kitchen, in front of eight women and a clerk, so the thing under classification is a man, and a man can be examined. **Both doors closed on that afternoon:** he will never ask to be classified, and she will never make the offer again, and there was a larger offer in the room that she did not make and would not describe and that he did not ask for. **A minute in four hands went out on the ordinary stream at four on the twenty-sixth and put his name in the middle of a record made by nine people he cannot argue with.** And on the twenty-ninth of December he wrote four sheets of plain paper at his own kitchen table, in his own hand, with his name at the top of the first sheet and at the foot of the last, and laid them into the keeping of that room, and **eight people signed a witness line under him, each of them carrying his name in a book of their own for four years, and there is a gap in the line where the fourth seat is.** **On the thirtieth, at six in the morning, the quarterly card came down in a different hand on different paper and printed a floor of 8,145 and put him at 8,145 on the nose, and for the first time since the thirtieth of July the ranks wall says no word about him at all**, because a man standing on the floor is a man in band. And at twenty to two that night, on the last night of the year, Wren Kallo — the officer who certified the composition of an address she lives at, in her own division, with a false attestation of the twenty-seventh of November filed under the standing papers and chosen to be left there — told a man she is going to speak to somebody she has not chosen yet, and went down sixty-one steps without a sound and out of a door, and **did not know who she was going to tell.**

**WHAT THE BATCH DID NOT DO, AND A LATER BATCH MUST NOT UNDO.** The classification is **described and not completed**; no finding has been entered. **No requisition was received.** A requisition was tabled and voided, and a void sheet is not a requisition. Nothing was routed, no grain was spent, no draw was made, no folio opened, no settle clock started and no settle date invented. **Lin Wei's file is still shut and still opens at 372.** The account of thirty years **remains called and unanswered** and will be called again at the valuation on the first of January at ten to nine. **The great offer is Chapter 486's; it was not made here and Anouk Ferreira is not diminished.** The ninth turned line, the seventh returned line, the bare slot on the Reading wall, the two unstamped reads in the check column of file 4,112, the day Ines Duarte lost and cannot date, and which record she was holding, were **not opened**. Ines Duarte is still not under his roof, still not on the list, still not one of the eight, and **nothing has been read in her rooms and nothing will be**. Wanda Rej is still in a ward with a leg that will not walk and the four pencil marks have gone nowhere. **Wren Kallo has told nobody yet. Volume 8 is the volume that finds out who.**

---

**WHAT CHANGED.** He stopped spending for eleven days to keep a woman who empties the bins on the fourth floor of his own building alive, and paid for it on a wall in front of strangers. **The plate went 8,184 → 8,139 (22 Nov, read 23rd) → 8,131 (29 Nov, read 30th): forty-five points down in one week and eight in the next, which is what a plate does when nothing is attested, and the two words the square has carried since the thirtieth of July came back and then stopped being news.** The account of thirty years fell under its floor and became callable at the valuation on the first of December at ten to nine, in hands he did not appoint, and the instrument that could call it belonged to a woman he had unprotected on purpose nine days earlier. Wanda Rej went down four flights of his own stair at two in the morning on the twenty-sixth and will not walk again, and she made him promise that the four marks on her hand do not go any further. **He broke the term of the ninth of July on a landing on Lantern Row on the twenty-seventh, and on the twenty-eighth an auditor read the classification in her own rooms and lost a day she cannot date, and kept the sheets in a drawer with a note on top of them dated that morning. On the thirtieth at night she read the classification back to him, out loud, for eleven minutes, and told him that nothing will ever be read in that room again — not to her and not by her.** And a Bureau officer at the same address wrote a false attestation out in her own hand on the twenty-seventh and has decided to leave it where it is, and he may not ask her one question about it.

**WHAT THE BATCH DID NOT DO, AND A LATER BATCH MUST NOT UNDO.** The classification is **described and not completed**; no finding has been entered. The Concil of the Nine was **not named** — it was named once in this volume, at 307. Anouk Ferreira is not on the page and does not enter until 344. No requisition was tabled and none was received, and a tabled-and-refused requisition would not be one either. Nothing was routed. **Lin Wei's file is still shut and still opens at 372.** No draw was made in ten chapters; the only grain that moved anywhere in this batch is one a woman refused on the floor of her own building. The seventh returned line, the ninth turned line and the bare slot on the Reading wall were not opened. Ines Duarte is still not under his roof, still not on the list, and is not one of the eight.

**WHAT CHANGED.** He did what he refused since Chapter 48, badly, in public, with witnesses. Three women attested, the weekly plate went 8,138 → 8,184 in green, he is seven and can never be unscored. He learned a movement needs a seal, nine seals at a year a use, his account handed by one, his brother's the match. Marguerite Vale put her books on a guild table at seven on the sixteenth and cleared her own table for the statement.

**§REPAIR — INDEPENDENT REVIEW OF THIS BATCH FOUND TWO FALSE ROWS AND ONE STRUCTURAL REGRESSION THE WHOLE GATE SET WAS BLIND TO. ALL THREE ARE FIXED. THIS BLOCK WAS WRITTEN BY A PASS THAT PUBLISHED A CLEAN SHEET, AND THE CLEAN SHEET WAS NOT CLEAN.**

**1. TWELVE LOST PARAGRAPH BREAKS, ALL NEW TO THIS BATCH. THE STRUCTURAL REGRESSION.** Every closing passage in 325, 326, 327, 328 and 330 had lost its blank lines, so paragraphs that should stand alone were hard-wrapped into their neighbours and markdown rendered them as one run-on block. **328 was the worst case: four separate closing beats in one paragraph, three of them deliberate one-line beats that could not render as one.** **0330 was the visible damage: its last two lines were the same sentence twice.** The 12-gram scan caught it five times over and the exact-duplicate gate could not see it at all. Restored: 325 x3, 326 x1, 327 x1, 328 x4, 330 x3. **Line-boundary gate now added above and returns 0.**

**2. THE 12-GRAM ROW WAS FALSE IN BOTH FIGURES.** It published 2,282 book-wide and NONE touching 321–330. Re-run against all 330 files on the whitespace/case/punct-significant tokenisation: **2,479 book-wide, and SIX touching 321–330, all of them in 0330** — five windows from the doubled closing sentence and one from an internal repeat of `out loud, against dates printed in rooms he was not in`. **After repair, re-run: 2,473 book-wide, NONE touching 321–330.** Both the false clean row and the stale book-wide figure are corrected above.

**3. THE `He nodded.` ROW WAS FALSE, AND THE FILLER WAS REAL.** It published that `He nodded.` and `He stared at her.` had been rewritten to unique forms. `He stared at her.` was genuinely 0 and that half held. **`He nodded.` appeared TEN times across the batch, once as a standalone one-line paragraph at 321:93.** `nodded` is named in AGENTS.md as mechanical filler. All ten are now rewritten into distinct beats and the row reads **0**. Each rewrite keeps what that paragraph was doing and adds the emotion the nod was standing in for.

**4. SECONDARY, ALSO REPAIRED.** `chapter-0325.md` stated the whole mechanism three times (31, 69, 117) in one chapter, which AGENTS.md prohibits; **line 69, the telling to the kitchen, is now a transmission and not a re-derivation** — it keeps the news for the kitchen and cuts the restatement of the rule Sowerby had given him twenty minutes earlier. Lines 31 and 117 stand: the first is the reveal and the third is a plain restatement at higher pressure that carries new information. **`chapter-0327.md:133` copied two sentences verbatim from lines 9 and 11 and landed as an unacknowledged copy; it is now a framed refrain that names what it returns to.**

**5. WORD COUNTS AND QUOTE BALANCE WERE ALSO STALE.** The published count row had six of ten figures out of date, and the quote row printed 82 for 325 against an actual 84. **Both were written before the last prose edit. This is the second time this file has recorded a self-reported row that was not re-run against the final files, and it is the reason the two false rows above went out.** Re-run and published above.

**6. THE ROOT CAUSE IS THE GATE SET, NOT THE WRITER. TWO NEW GATES, BOTH NOW IN THE RECORD ABOVE.** **Every duplicate gate in this project compares whole-paragraph identity. Two paragraphs merged into one are, by definition, not two paragraphs, so no whole-paragraph gate can see a lost break. A line-boundary gate is a different gate and is now run.** The same blind spot hid the doubled sentence inside a single 0330 paragraph. **The lesson this file has now earned twice: a gate that compares a unit the defect does not respect will return clean forever.**

**NO PLANNED PLOT MOVED. No card, no day spine, no movement, and no chapter was rewritten from the top. Nine files changed, all in 321–330, none in 329. Chapter 329 is the scene and was not touched at all.**

**1. THE CALENDAR DISAGREES WITH ITSELF BY ONE DAY, AND IT HAS SINCE VOLUME 5.** The head block of
`state/current.md` §3.2 prints **August 1 a Tuesday**, which puts August's Thursdays on the third, tenth,
seventeenth and twenty-fourth — correct — and prints **September's Thursdays as the seventh, fourteenth,
twenty-first and twenty-eight**, which cannot both be true. **A month is thirty days and advances the weekday
by two, so August 1 a Tuesday makes September 1 a Thursday and puts September's Thursdays on the first,
eighth, fifteenth, twenty-second and twenty-ninth.** (The printed list is the one you get from a September
whose first falls on a Friday, which would want August 1 to be a Wednesday.)
**RULING: the page governs and both printed lists stand exactly as written, because eleven closed chapters
depend on the September list and re-deriving them is not available.**
**CONSEQUENCE, AND IT IS BINDING: Volume 7 names NO DAY OF THE WEEK AT ALL — not Monday, not Friday, not
Saturday, and not Thursday.** Write the date. **A batch that genuinely needs a Thursday records the
discrepancy in its review file and does not resolve it in the prose.** This is a standing open item for the
Volume 7 close audit, not a thing to be fixed in a batch.

**2. `bible/power-system.md`'s BAND TABLE DOES NOT MATCH THE PRINTED PLATE AND THE PAGE GOVERNS.** The table
puts rank five at 8,000 and rank seven at 55,000 grains a week. **The plate in this book prints 8,138 and
`state/continuity.md` has carried *band five on the plate, band six on a piece of paper* for three volumes.**
**PROPOSED CANON CLARIFICATION, FLAGGED AND NOT ADOPTED: the table is illustrative and every band threshold
in the prose is read off a card on the ranks wall printed in the chapter that uses it.** **No batch may derive
a threshold from the table, and no batch edits `bible/power-system.md` to fix it.** Recorded here so that a
later reader who trusts the table does not silently contradict three closed volumes.

**3. THE GATE LESSON IS NOW FOUR VOLUMES OLD AND IT IS THE STANDING INSTRUCTION FOR THE NEXT PHASE.**
Three consecutive Volume 6 batches reported a clean gate row that was not clean, and **every one of those
rows was written by the same pass that wrote the prose.** Batch 0005's own block claimed a first pass of 137
and a final of 0; an independent run of the committed files found 9. **A gate record is a claim about a
file. Re-run it from outside the pass and let the re-run outrank the record.** The Volume 7 Batch 0001
prompt carries this and instructs the batch to print the figure a command returns against the files after
its last edit, with the tokenisation and the scope stated beside it.

**AND THE TWO THAT ARE NOT NEW BUT ARE STILL THE EXPENSIVE ONES:** **never run a paragraph-length splitter
over prose containing dialogue** — Volume 6's Batch 0004 ran two and lost three of four days and restored a
closed chapter from git twice — **and treat every hit for `it went on a long time` or `afterwards` as an
unwritten scene.** Three summarised scenes shipped in Volume 6, at 279, 289 and 299, and the third passed
the gate that had recorded the first two as clean.

**Ten chapters, twenty-nine days. A month is thirty days and has no thirty-first. **August 1 is a Tuesday,
August's Thursdays are the third, the tenth, the seventeenth and the twenty-fourth — the thirty-first is a
phantom — and September's are the seventh, the fourteenth, the twenty-first and the twenty-eighth.** Day map:
291 the 2nd, **the two instruments at one door and three names, her own first** · 292 the 5th, **THE FIFTH
DOCUMENT, and it is a book** · 293 the 8th, **a pencil note in the margin of a quarter-book that is closing**
· 294 the 12th, **Beatrix Halm gets seven dates and the guild says what it will call** · 295 the 15th, **the
pen on a chain, refused, and a written undertaking taken in exchange** · 296 the 16th, **HE DESCRIBES THE
ACCOUNT AND SHE PRICES IT AT SEVEN — the irreversible decision** · 297 the 17th and 19th, **the keys change
and the household is frozen for three days** · 298 the 30th, **the band goes to 8,144 and the book closes
blank** · 299 the night of the 30th, **VOLUME 6'S FIFTH AND FINAL SCENE** · 300 the 1st of October, **the
round at eight, the mark at ten to nine, and the close.**

### What the gates do not tell you

**This batch's irreversible decision is the one the volume turns on: he describes the reservoir to a keeper
instead of asking her for it, and she prices it at the figure of the household.** Everything else in the ten
chapters is machinery for that, and the mechanical pass found none of it.

**The batch's strongest turn is a woman telling a man he is hiding behind a word.** Runa Sato says on the
second of September that the promise of the twenty-seventh of August covers merit and not persons, and he
agrees, and gives the real reason, which is nine seconds on the first of August and a house eight women live
in. **The routing has still not happened and the promise has now been tested in public and survived on the
honest reason rather than the convenient one.**

**The batch's cost is a price.** Chapter 296 puts a dated, initialled line in a real book with an address and
a figure of seven on it and no name anywhere, and it cannot be called back, and he asked for it. **In about
four years the woman standing in that room will be answerable for seven people she has never met.**

>
> **THE 12-GRAM SCAN RAN FIRST, AGAINST ALL 290 CHAPTERS, BEFORE THE 8-WORD SENTENCE SCAN, AND IT WAS RUN
> REPEATEDLY.** **The first pass returned 131.** Batch 0003's first pass returned 387 and Batch 0002's returned
> 44, so a large first figure is this book's normal condition and not a surprise. **All 131 were cut and the
> final figure is 14, not 0.** **THOSE 14 ARE TWO REFRAINS AND ARE NAMED BELOW, AND THE EARLIER VERSION OF
> THIS BLOCK REPORTED THEM AS ZERO, WHICH WAS WRONG.** The reviewer's first pass over Batch 0004 caught the
> error and it was corrected here on the review's finding. **THE TOKENISATION IS PART OF THE FIGURE AND IS
> STATED.** The figure is the **whitespace split with case and punctuation significant**, the same gate Batch

> lower-cased, punctuation stripped to spaces, 12-gram — returns 6 for this batch, and every one of the six is

> 271; and *at twenty-five minutes past four on the twentieth of July*, in 287 and 288 and, before them, 264 and
> 274). **A date is a fact about this world and not a sentence, and two of those facts have to appear more than
> once.** Both figures are reported because a 12-gram number without its tokenisation is a number nobody else
> can reproduce.
>
> **A LATER BATCH THAT RUNS THE 12-GRAM SCAN MUST SUBTRACT THE NAMED REFRAINS BY HAND AND SAY SO.** Two are
> standing in this volume: the clerk and the shelf in 285, and the bottom of the street in 288. **A scan that
> cannot tell a refrain from an accident will keep reporting clean batches as dirty and dirty batches as clean,
> and that is the failure this row now exists to prevent.**
>
> **A QUOTE-BALANCE ROW IS IN THIS BLOCK AND IT IS NOT IN THE STANDARD LIST, AND THIS BATCH IS THE REASON.**
> Batch 0003 added the row because a machine paragraph-splitter broke four speeches. **This batch ran a
> paragraph-splitter of its own over prose that contains dialogue and it destroyed the dialogue structure in
> all ten chapters, twice, and ten chapters had to be rewritten from the drafts.** The gate below is a
> whole-file count of quotation marks and it returns **10 of 10 even**, which is the cheapest possible test and
> the one that catches the failure. **A later batch that splits paragraphs programmatically must check
> quotation balance before it checks anything else, and must not run the splitter on dialogue at all.**
>
> **THE REVIEW OF THIS BATCH FOUND THREE OF THIS BLOCK'S OWN ROWS OVER-REPORTING CLEAN.** The 12-gram row said
> 0 and is 14 · the 8-word-opening row said 0 and was 4 · the nobody row said 9 from a case-sensitive count and
> is 16 by the same tokenisation, or 9 case-insensitively. **A row on a gate block is a claim about a file and
> not a result, and a reviewer's re-run of it outranks whatever the block says about itself.**

**Ten chapters, twenty-three days. A month is thirty days and has no thirty-first. July 1 is a Saturday, so
July's Thursdays are the sixth, the thirteenth, the twentieth and the twenty-seventh; **August 1 is a Tuesday,
August's Thursdays are the third, the tenth, the seventeenth, the twenty-fourth and the thirty-first, and
September's are the seventh, the fourteenth, the twenty-first and the twenty-eighth.** Day map: 281 the 10th,
**the movement opens, the third ask, and a keeper puts him out of her room** · 282 the 13th, **Beatrix Halm and
what a security that does not cover costs, and the condition she gives him** · 283 the 16th, **THE DESK —
Ines Duarte at a desk where she is the subject, and the term goes onto a file** · 284 the 19th, **Petra Ilyin
and a quarter round whose twentieth address is his, with the figure seven under it** · 285 the 22nd,
**Wren Kallo's sixth telling on an ordinary afternoon, and the word *unusual* goes off the record** · 286 the
24th Thursday, **THE FIGURE — eight thousand one hundred and thirty-eight, initialled and not signed** · 287 the
27th, **a broker opens a drawer, takes nothing out of it, and refuses the routing out loud in her own rooms** ·
288 the 29th,
**the panel, and he is at the bottom of the street** · 289 the 30th, **the card at six, five under, and
Sun Meiling's scene** · 290 the 1st of September, **the certification, the bar, and Sun Meiling in ink.**

### What the gates do not tell you

**This batch's failure mode was a tool, not a sentence.** Two scripts were run over the prose to find
paragraphs over ninety words, and both of them took dialogue apart: one stripped the opening quotation mark off
continuation paragraphs and the other moved attribution clauses out of order, and between them they destroyed the
dialogue structure in all ten chapters. **Chapter 280, which is Batch 0003's closed chapter, was caught by the
same script and restored from git.** Ten chapters were rewritten from the drafts, once, by hand, and the third
of the four days this batch cost went into that rather than into the plot. The repair is recorded in
`reviews/volume-06/batch-0004.md` §3 so that Batch 0005 does not run the same script.

**The batch's strongest turn is a woman asking for a thing she has already been refused and being given it.**
Petra Ilyin asks to be the one who knocks at his door on the first of October, and he agrees in four seconds,
and she tells him it is the worst thing anybody has said to her in eleven years, and it is.

**The batch's cost is a bar.** Sun Meiling goes into ink on a certificate that he certified, in his own hand,
over a defect he described in his own hand, and she is neither grateful nor ungrateful about it, and she asked
for it on the fifth of August in front of six people, and he has been ungrateful on her behalf since the ninth
and will not be forgiven for it.

> **THE 12-GRAM SCAN RAN FIRST, AGAINST ALL 280 CHAPTERS, BEFORE THE 8-WORD SENTENCE SCAN, and it was run
> repeatedly rather than once.** It returned **387** on the first pass. Every one of those was a real defect —

> 266 word for word, quoted the reconciliation from 268 and the notice from 270, and restated two rules from
> itself. **Three hundred and seventy-nine were cut and the final figure is 2.** Batch 0002 shipped 13 and
> Batch 0001 shipped 29. **The rule for a later batch: a figure quoted from an earlier chapter is not free, and
> a rule stated in this batch must not be restated in this batch.**
>
> **THE 12-GRAM GATE IS A PREFIX OR A WHOLE PARAGRAPH, AND THE TWO ANSWER DIFFERENTLY.** The gate that
> mattered — an exact duplicate *paragraph*, at either floor, across 261–280 — returns **1**, and it is
> `He sat down.` at 262 and 263, which are not this batch's chapters. A first-6-words-prefix test returns 10 and
> a first-4-words-prefix test returns 68, and every one of those 68 is an ordinary English opener and not a
> repeated sentence. **Both figures are recorded below and neither is a duplicate paragraph.**

> **AN INDEPENDENT REVIEW RAN AFTER THIS BATCH AND REPAIRED IT. THE REPAIR IS ON DISK.** Five defects were
> returned and **four of the five were in this block and in `reviews/volume-06/batch-0003.md`, not in the
> chapters** — the `nobody` row below reported **0 where the text says 5**, §2's day map skipped a day in the
> Term column and carried the first of August's figures against a row labelled *30 Jul n / 1 Aug*, and the bare
> table's day count drifted between 273 and 280. **The fifth was reader-visible prose: `She waited.` stood in
> Ch 279 twice, 124 lines apart, both as a beat before she speaks, and it sat below the gate's four-word floor so
> no gate in this block could ever have caught it.** **Four one-line repairs went onto the page and no plot
> moved: 272:123 twenty-two → twenty-three days · 276:143 twenty-nine → twenty-six days · 279:27 *She waited.*
> → *She did not take it back.* · 280:11 twenty-seventh → twenty-sixth day.** **Two carried figures are named
> and left standing rather than invented away: `270:111` prints the term one day under §2 because it counts
> elapsed and Chapter 270 is Batch 0002's closed chapter, and `276:3`'s saucer count matches no counter in the
> book and depends on an anchor at `266:101`, so nobody may move it without moving both.** Full record,
> including every number this block now carries and the two this block previously got wrong, is
> `reviews/volume-06/batch-0003.md` §7.
>
> **THE DAY COUNTS IN THIS VOLUME ARE INCLUSIVE — THE DAY OF THE ANCHOR IS DAY ONE — AND THAT RULE IS NOW
> WRITTEN DOWN WHERE IT WAS NOT BEFORE.** `reviews/volume-06/batch-0003.md` §2 carries the rebuilt map, with
> every column derived, **two rows for Ch 271** (the thirtieth's night, and the first at dawn), and eight of its
> figures checked against numbers that are actually printed in the chapters. **A batch that counts anything
> against a date in this volume counts inclusively or states that it is not.**

**Ten chapters, one a day from the night of the thirtieth of July to the ninth of August. A month is thirty
days and has no thirty-first. July 1 is a Saturday, so **July's Thursdays are the sixth, the thirteenth, the
twentieth and the twenty-seventh**, the thirtieth of July is a Sunday, **August 1 is a Tuesday and August 3 is
a Thursday.** Day map: 271 the 30th at night and the 1st at dawn, **the cage and the back booth, Ch 269's owed
scene, and Ines Duarte's return with a correction filed the night before** · 272 the 1st, **the hundred and
thirty-fourth mark, and seven sworn on a production line** · 273 the 2nd, **the composition read at seven, the
seventh line blank, and the unnamed seventh worked out on a wall** · 274 the 3rd Thursday, **the
reconciliation, signed, and three books that do not see each other** · 275 the 4th, **the dead list is quiet,
Beatrix is told what her tail was bought with, and the annual sitting gets a date** · 276 the 5th, **Sun
Meiling, the certificate, and a request refused in front of six women** · 277 the 6th, **Cordwain, the stale
paper, and the address that is not a sleeping body** · 278 the 7th, **the district counter, two roads, one door,
and Wren Kallo reading him** · 279 the 8th, **Wren Kallo's scene** · 280 the 9th, **the seventh step down,
*do not write it*, and a sentence with no name in it.**

### What the gates do not tell you

**This batch's failure mode was restatement, and it was worse than Batch 0002's dates and Batch 0001's
arithmetic, because it is invisible to every gate in the block above until you run the 12-gram scan — and the
12-gram scan returned 387 before it returned 2.** Nine separate passages were lifted word for word from an
earlier chapter into a later chapter in the same batch, including a page of Chapter 269 quoted back at the
reader four paragraphs after it was set up and the instrument from 266 quoted in full in 276 by a woman asking
for it to be read back. **A batch that quotes an earlier chapter is quoting itself, because the book is one
voice; the fix is to write the rule once and then describe the second instance in different words.**

**The second failure was mechanical and it is worth recording because the gate did not catch it either.** A
paragraph-length splitter was run across the batch and it broke long speeches at sentence boundaries, which
opened and closed quotation marks that did not belong there. **Three separate repair passes made it worse and
the chapters had to be rewritten from the drafts.** The final gate block includes a quote-balance row that
does not exist in the standard list, and **a later batch that splits paragraphs programmatically must check
quotation balance before it checks anything else.**

## Volume 6, Batch 0002 — RETAINED GATE RECORD (Chapters 261–270). SUPERSEDED BY BATCH 0003 AS TO POSITION.

> **This block supersedes Batch 0001's as the live gate record.** Batch 0001's block is retained below it,
> superseded as to position. **Every number below was produced by command against the files as they stand
> after Batch 0002's last edit, and every row says what was run and what was read, because a gate that returns
> zero is a claim to be tested and not a result to be reported.** The full record is
> `reviews/volume-06/batch-0002.md`.
>
> **THE 12-GRAM SCAN RAN FIRST, AGAINST ALL 270 CHAPTERS, BEFORE THE 8-WORD SENTENCE SCAN.** It returned
> **44** on the first pass. Thirty-one were cut and the final figure is **13**, against Batch 0001's recorded
> 29. Six of the thirty-one were self-repetition *inside this batch*, including one rule that was stated
> correctly in 265 and then restated from memory in 266 and was wrong in the restatement. **That is the
> failure mode of this batch and it is the inverse of Batch 0001's, which was dates. Derive a rule from the
> sentence that states it, never from a memory of the sentence that stated it.**
>
> **THE WEEKDAY GATE FAILED ON THE FIRST PASS, AND FIXING IT BROKE THE CALENDAR.** Eight chapters named
> Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, Friday, Saturday or Sunday. Every one was replaced — and in five places the
> replacement word was *Thursday*, which put a Thursday on the twenty-third of July, and **the twenty-third is
> a Sunday.** An independent review found it on a second pass and every affected sentence now carries a date or
> the institution's own word. **The gate cannot catch this class: "only Thursday is named" passes precisely when
> the word is misused.** A later batch that hits the gate must check the surviving Thursdays against the
> calendar, not just count them.

**Ten chapters, one a day from the twenty-first to the thirtieth of July 2026 in the book's calendar. A month
is thirty days and has no thirty-first, and the thirtieth is the last working day. Day map: 261 the 21st,
**Isolde Marchetti's scene, the verse, the household becomes public** · 262 the 22nd, **the instrument comes
back and it no longer needs Ines's hand** · 263 the 23rd, **the answer song, and Agnete Lindqvist goes out**
· 264 the 24th, **Rask names the caller and he asks her to reconcile** · 265 the 25th, **Cordwain, ten to
three, the two halves** · 266 the 26th, **the form behind the pencil row, and he signs it** · 267 the 27th
Thursday, **the pass along the ranks wall and Beatrix's guild hold in a public shade** · 268 the 28th,
**Marguerite comes back with a bag** · 269 the 29th, **Nadia Ferrous in the lift, the position** · 270 the
30th, **the quarter, the band list, and a notice with a date on it.**

> **AN INDEPENDENT REVIEW RAN AFTER THIS BATCH AND REPAIRED IT. THE REPAIR IS ON DISK.** Nine defects were
> returned, four of them reader-visible contradictions — **the band posting dated three different ways, Ch 265
> breaking its own rule four paragraphs after stating it, "that is tomorrow" in 269 for a Thursday five days
> away, an instrument called signed in 267 after he refused to sign it in 262, and a July date counted from a
> Sunday** — plus the weekday collision above. **No plot moved: no event, no decision, no figure a chapter
> depends on, no scene, and nothing written outside 261–270.** What moved is dates, three counts, one doubled
> quotation mark, one banned construction, and the four-day wait in 266, which bought nothing under the rule
> the chapter states and now says so. The full record, including the corrections to *this* file's figures,
> is `reviews/volume-06/batch-0002.md` §7.

**A rule invented and then restated from memory was this batch's failure, and it is not a class any gate
counts.** Three instruments are introduced in ten chapters — a change of auditor of record, the validity of
a hearing paper, and the day a division acknowledges a change — and each was written once correctly and then
paraphrased wrongly later. All three are fixed. **The full before-and-after table with anchors is
`reviews/volume-06/batch-0002.md` §2.**

**Eight hand-traced date and count errors were found in this batch's own first draft**, all fixed, and the
day map with the full arithmetic is at `reviews/volume-06/batch-0002.md` §2. Two were contradictions with an
earlier chapter rather than arithmetic errors: *"Thirty years indoors"* invented for Agnete against her canon
eleven, and **the Bellhouse house at four hundred people in 261 and nine hundred in 263.**

### Canon this batch makes, in

1. **A head may diminish his own household on his own sworn answer, with a week and a broker's signature,
   and no auditor at all.** The instrument a head may give up a room was always this. **Marguerite Vale told
   him the truth on the seventh of July and let him stand on a false reason for fifteen days, on purpose,
   because if she had taken the reason away he would have used it before the seventh of August.** He
   declined it, it died of its own week on the twenty-ninth, and on the twenty-ninth he wrote the refusal out
   late and in his own hand, which makes it a refusal rather than a silence.
2. **The price of giving up a room is that Ines Duarte goes off the paper forever, and there is nothing to
   contest.** Five in August, no seventh anywhere, no row to dispute, and every figure he has ever sworn
   matches every figure anybody holds. **It is the only thing anybody in this book has ever wanted, and it
   costs the only woman he has been protecting.** Sun Meiling worked this out in ninety seconds at a table
   and said *it is not about me, it has been about her since the seventh of July and I have been pleased about
   it.*
3. **An impediment on a certificate is a bar on the certificate, not a bar to certifying, and it comes off
   by removing the person it is on.** A change of auditor of record takes effect on the day the division
   acknowledges it, the division acknowledges on the first, and a month has thirty days and no thirty-first.
   **He signed it on the twenty-sixth. Sun's row goes into ink on the first of September, not the first of
   August**, and the price is the office.
4. **A called standing is answered at a valuation, and a valuation is a mark at ten to nine on the first.**
   The standing lodged on the seventh of June was called on the twentieth at twenty-five minutes past four,
   while he was swearing seven in a room at Cobb Street. Rask told him on the thirteenth that it would take
   four quarters. **It took seventeen days from the day she told him.** The floor went up on the thirtieth
   from 8,141 to **8,142** and he is **four under**, and the mark that keeps the account alive is the
   valuation at which the standing is answered.
5. **The quarterly bands go up on the last working day of the quarter, in a shade the square has not had, by
   a different office and a different hand from the Thursday crew.** The wall said **out of band** in front of
   about two hundred people and a foreman read it out loud, because a wall is read before it is painted.
6. **A published security binds everybody who takes from the account afterwards, which is why a guild posts
   its hold.** Beatrix Halm's name is on the ranks wall in a public shade from the twenty-seventh of July.
   She wanted it left, not struck, and then she went and stood under the wall to have the song over it.
7. **A hearing paper runs from the month it was answered in to the end of the month after it.** Runa Sato's
   paper was answered on the twenty-fourth of June and went stale at midnight on the thirtieth of July.
   **She was offered the conversion once a week for four weeks by the same instrument the guild reached for
   over Beatrix and she turned it down herself, out loud, in front of a man who had come to buy it.**
8. **A reconciliation reads the real book and the broker's own book and publishes where they do not agree.
   Solveig Rask, 42, names the caller and it is the broker's device, and a device can be cut again by anybody
   who has held the pen.** The broker's working book and the key to the chained room were in a bag in two
   rooms from the fourteenth of July to the twenty-eighth. The page comes on **the third of August**, because
   the last Thursday in July was the twenty-seventh.
9. **Rask moves from a room to a name.** Four hundred and nineteen days to the annual sitting on the
   twenty-fourth of July. She will not price the reservoir this month or this year, **but she is now one
   sentence from describing it** — thirty-one lines in a column and, under the column, one page with a figure
   on it and two letters at the foot that are his. **She said that on the twenty-fourth and it is closer
   than it was on the thirteenth.**
10. **A room that hears Isolde Marchetti agrees, and she is never in a room without it happening.** Her cost
    is printed on the page for the first time. **In eight feet by seven over a stage door with eight bulbs lit
    out of eleven, it did not happen, because she decided that it would not.** She is 39 and her scene is
    Chapter 261 and it is written.
11. **A verse is an instruction and a song is an argument, and the difference is that a song cannot be
    refused by the person it is about.** Twenty pence at a box office, a man in a good coat at the office end
    of a bar on the eighteenth, a standing commission with no date on the card, and *say it exactly, and do
    not change the number*. The twenty-third's answer named the block by its two names for itself and put
    **34-6-118** into nine hundred mouths. **The household has been public since Chapter 261.**
12. **Nadia Ferrous is the only member of a household whose thing is a book.** She has been writing a second
    copy out by hand one page a night since the nineteenth of July, eleven pages, **the eleventh of which she
    left blank and put into the book anyway**, because she does not skip a day. **She is the first partner in
    this book who has asked to be in something rather than on something.**
13. **Agnete Lindqvist went out on the twenty-third of July, for the second time in eleven years, and on the
    thirtieth she was in a Square in daylight.** Wren Kallo wrote the date against it in her own book, in her
    own hand, which is the first date anybody in this city has ever written against anything that has
    happened to her. **That is a door, not a protection, and Wren said so.**

### Canon this batch makes, out

- **Ines Duarte does not appear.** She is not in this batch and she is not on the page. **The term agreed in
  the dark on the ninth of July holds and has held for twenty-one days**, and a notice came to Ferrier Street
  on the thirtieth requiring **the former auditor of record to attend on the second of August at ten** — put
  there by a division reading a number off a wall, **not by her.** She comes back on her own terms or not at
  all, and **Chapter 271 is hers.**
- **The hundred and thirty-fourth mark is not entered in this batch.** It falls at ten to nine on the first of
  August, one day outside a batch that runs one day per chapter from the twenty-first. Everything that
  decides it is on the page. **It belongs to Chapter 271.**
- **Wren Kallo's finding is advanced and not resolved.** Agnete's date is in Wren's own book and Wren's held
  name and who she has told are untouched. **Ines Duarte's review is not resolved.** **Beatrix Halm's hearing
  is not resolved** and the guild's hold is still on it. **Runa Sato's hearing goes stale and is not
  converted and is not resolved** — it is now a date somebody once mentioned.
- **Sun Meiling's row is not counted.** It is not counted because a change of auditor has to be acknowledged
  on the first of a month and a month has no thirty-first and the first is a valuation morning. **The first
  of September is on the page.**
- **The reservoir is not priced, not described, not classified and not spent. No system panel is due.**
- **The sixth line on the page in 250 is still not a debt.**
- **Nothing is routed. Ines Duarte is the first woman he would route it through and the routing has not
  happened and it is not resolved in ten chapters, as it must not be.**
