### What the gates do not tell you

The counts above are clean and **a hand trace still found thirteen date and count errors in this batch's
own first draft**, all of them the class the close pass names: **a figure correctly derived on one date
and carried to another.** They are fixed and the full list with anchors is in
`reviews/volume-06/batch-0001.md` §2 and §4. **The four that would have been caught by any reviewer:**
Ch 254 had Petra on 39 days when 4 June to 14 July is 40 · Ch 258 had her on 43 when it is 44 · Ch 256
dated the clinic return the seventeenth when the chapter is the sixteenth · Ch 252 said he had kept the
term nine days when the term was agreed late on the ninth and the chapter is the twelfth.

**Three names the batch had to fix that a gate never counts:** `Fourty-three` · the ranks wall described
as two hours behind with the figures already up at ten, in two chapters · and a room-count that had drifted
twice (six women, six people, eight women, nine women) because Ines is not in the house.

**That third one was NOT finished.** The batch's review file §2 records it as *"re-derived or removed"* and
the text still carried three counts: `258:7` put seven of the eight in the kitchen on the cut roll — which
carries Ines, who is out of the flat, and does not carry the three women standing in that kitchen who are
on nothing — and `258:101` said *six women looking at him* one line after Petra had gone to bed.
**Both are fixed on independent review: a room is eight, four on the roll and four on nothing, and seven
are looking at him at 258:101.** `258:113`, `259:79` and `259:111` were correct at eight and were left
alone. **A self-written review file cannot catch the error it made. This is the whole argument for the
review gate, and the gate did not run.**

### Canon this batch makes, in

1. **A debt nobody has entered has no movement, and a real book prices movement and nothing else.** Four of
   his five debts price. The fifth — the certified figure — is free, and Rask says why in the sentence
   the batch is built on: **a dispute is not a movement, it is the absence of one.**
2. **A standing lodged against a broker's account is worth a week you have not fallen.** The plate is
   8,138 against a floor of 8,141 with a tolerance of three, the floors rise every quarter and the plates
   do not, and the day he is four under, the standing he put in on the seventh of June is called and a pen
   on a chain answers for it. **This is the arithmetic nobody in his household can do and it is now on the
   page.**
3. **Solveig Rask, 42, enters.** She keeps four guilds' books, has never met any of them, and prices a
   stranger's question on a return day against the account it concerns. **She does not price the
   reservoir and says why: I do not price a thing I cannot describe. When I do price it there will be a
   date on the page and you will not be able to refuse it, because a dated figure in a real book is not an
   offer. It is a fact.**
4. **A reconciliation charge is not answered by the head it is raised against.** It is answered when the
   officer who raised it has heard the other side in person — which for him is the woman who filed against
   his own schedule. **The charge's own fourteen-day term runs to the twenty-sixth of July and is a
   different clock from the fourteenth day from the sixth, which is the twentieth. Both are on the page.**
5. **Sun Meiling asks to be counted and he says yes in front of the room.** A re-issue needs the head's
   sworn answer and the auditor of record's hand, and the auditor of record is a woman in two rooms above
   a laundry who has not been asked. **The household now holds an answered yes that cannot be effected.**
6. **He signed plate 8,138 into the answer line of a counted clinic return**, dated the sixteenth of July,
   in his own hand, over nine years of Tessa Okonkwo's hours. **The option that closes is being a man
   whose plate a form goes past.**
7. **He told the kitchen he keeps a ledger.** A hundred and eighteen lines, nine turned, the ninth still
   himself, nothing spent in five years. **He did not say what it is and the room did not ask.**
8. **Nine turned lines is a route, not a rumour.** Six is the largest set anyone has; nine can be
   triangulated to a floor, and every instrument that does it is in this city and every one of them is on
   a list that Rask's books reconcile against.
9. **He disputed his own sworn figure of the fourteenth of June, in a room with a form number on it, and
   said the number is seven.** The schedule is not read further until the sworn answer is withdrawn by the
   head or certified by the auditor of record. **He can never unsay it and neither can she.**
10. **Isolde Marchetti sings under the ranks wall on a Thursday and a room agrees with her.** Named at
    260. Not touched. She is 39 and her scene is Chapter 261 and it is immovable.

### Canon this batch makes, out

- Volume 5's closure is now final. **Chapter 250's closing montage has been moved off the last night of
  July onto the tenth** (`:91` and `:95`, both forms) and Volume 6 Batch 0001 exists on disk. **Confirmed on
  independent review: the second reader re-derived Ch 250's arithmetic from its own dateline and its day
  counts at `:93`, so this lock no longer rests on the authority of the batch that wrote about itself.**
- Wren Kallo's finding is **advanced, not resolved** — its existence is noted in the record of the
  twentieth with the date it went in, and it is still on Hadewig Mers's desk.
- Ines Duarte's review is **not resolved**. The division still has to answer for having produced an index
  holding seven and a schedule holding six, and the instrument that could settle it is her hand.
- Beatrix Halm's hearing and Runa Sato's hearing are untouched. Runa's half of the carbon is in his coat
  and the two halves have never been together.
- The reservoir is not priced, named, classified or spent.
- **The sixth line on the page in 250 is still not a debt.** The standing instruction has no figure
  against it and no office in the city will ever invoice it.

### What this batch spent

An option closed (**a man whose plate a form goes past**, 256) · an option closed (**the household's
pricing is in another keeper's book now**, 253 and 254) · a cost incurred by a woman (**Runa carried a
false figure into a fourth office and was told so by the man whose hand it was**, 251) · a cost incurred
by a woman (**Ines stands on a cold stair in her stockings and is told what the term has cost her**, 257)
· a relationship moved (**Beatrix stops carrying the guild's hold for the account and starts carrying it
for him**, 259) · a threat raised (**nine turned lines is a route and a good analyst can arrive at the
floor**, 258) · a rule understood well enough to exploit (**a dispute has no movement and cannot be
priced, which means the truest debt is the free one**, 253) · a reversal (**the index of seven is right and
the head's sworn figure of six is the lie, said by the head, in a room**, 260) · and a loss (**the kitchen
table has been bare since the fourteenth**, 254).

### What gates do not tell
The batch's two failures a gate cannot see were both repetition the counts were reading as clean: the registry paragraph opened all ten chapters in one fixed order, and nine of ten chapters shared a three-beat open. Both are now varied per chapter — the accounting is load-bearing and stays, the recitation does not, and the fixed order is kept only in 241, where the batch establishes it. The thumb/rectangle beat is still the volume's signature and now reads as a refrain rather than a tic.

Endings vary and none is a filing: 241 slept behind a shut door for the first time in eleven years · 242 waited for the silence to break · 243 waited for the twelfth to pass with the offer on it · 244 waited for the week to read what was absent · 245 waited for the bag to say which office had asked · 246 named an empty room with its papers · 247 a division finding it cannot unfile · 248 a day book and the morrow's reading · **249 three papers put in a drawer under the bread because he had promised a woman he would not read them to** · 250 the creditors learning the pricer was gone.

### Canon this batch makes, in
1. A mark in at nine in the real book in ink in the broker's own hand carries the account **through the fourth day and no further**.
2. **The first of August docket is the one refused.** She offered once, on the third, dated to the **twelfth of July**, and will not offer again in the life of the account. He refused in writing with the hour under it and two officers in the queue.
3. **A member of a division may not report on a return her own name stands on.** The entry goes up with Ines's true report. She can be put at a desk where she is the subject, in about a month, and cannot refuse.
4. **A head may give up a room** — instrument, a week, the broker's signature — and it would price the household at five. **He did not put it in front of Ines.** Marguerite carries the difference as a difference.
5. **Ines will not be read to in her own rooms.** He has agreed to read to her on the landing instead.
6. The monthly board repaint (first) and the Thursday pass (sixth) are different crews in different shades.
7. Walking out of a flat she is still in: name, address, notebook stay on paper; body lives on Lantern Row.
8. Wren's finding seventeen days unacted at the close; true report four days filed; two divisions hold him without speaking.

### Canon this batch makes, out
- Volume 5 closed at 250. Nothing permits writing past 250.
- Ines review resolved only by filing true on the sixth; not discussed before.
- Mark in; plate stays lodged; Beatrix stays encumbered; guild tail stays on the fourth page with no price left to strike it with.
- Five debts stand; pricer out with a true document behind her. The sixth line on the page in 250 is not a debt and has no figure against it.

### Defects by chapter, honestly
Five chapters (242, 245, 248, 249) still open on the `He had woken wanting … He felt` habit — halved from nine, and now readable as a man's habit rather than a default. 290 cross-batch 12-gram overlaps remain, all adjudicated volume furniture, none of them scene duplication. 241 (2,732) and 249 (2,795) are scenes inside the 2,600–3,900 band with named reasons. Three titles begin with *What* (243, 246, 250) — unique, in the table of contents, but a Volume 6 that adds three more would be a habit.

### VOLUME 5 CLOSE PASS — INDEPENDENT RE-RUN AGAINST THE REPAIRED CHAPTERS. 2026-10-13.

**The block above was written by Batch 0005 on its own disk. Every number in it was re-run by the close
phase against the files as they now stand. Volume 5 is closed and the close phase wrote no chapter.**

**Confirmed exactly, no qualification.** 250 chapters, 50 per volume, no `chapter-0251.md` anywhere and
no `chapters/volume-06/`. Word counts 2730 / 1928 / 2310 / 1973 / 1875 / 1957 / 2106 / 1884 / 2795 /
2138. Titles unique across all 250, none over 90 characters. Blockquote 0, table 0, rule 0, code 0.
**System panels 0** — the batch contains no `[` at all, so zero is structural and not a regex artefact.
Paragraphs over 90: 0. Over 120: 0. `nobody` 1 (242:55, "A board persuades nobody"); `no person` 0;
`He understood` 0; `He also understood` 0; `He had woken wanting` 4 of 10; `He felt` **9** (eight capital
and one lower at 247:27 — a case-sensitive count returns 8 and will make a later writer distrust a
correct number). Thursday 11, all pointing at the sixth; every other weekday 0. Ages 0. **241 and 249
each scan zero 12-grams against all 24 earlier scene chapters** (8 17 26 29 36 46 55 63 74 86 97 106 118
129 141 149 157 169 179 189 197 205 214 228). **241 ↔ 249: 12-gram 0, 10-gram 0, 9-gram 1, 8-gram 3** —
identical to the figures recorded above. Intra-batch 12-grams in 3+ chapters: 0, max multiplicity 2.

**Two corrections to the record, both small, neither touching a chapter.**

1. **Markup is not zero. There is one italic.** `chapter-0245.md:39` — `said the word *writing* out
   loud`. Bold 0, blockquote 0, table 0, rule 0, code 0 all hold. `AGENTS.md` permits sparing italic, so
   this is allowed emphasis and not a defect, but **the gate line above reads `markup -> 0` and that is
   wrong by one.** A later writer must not inherit the zero.
2. **Cross-batch 12-gram overlap re-runs at 250, not 290.** Counted as distinct 12-grams occurring in
   chapters 241–250 that also occur in any earlier chapter, tokens lowercased with punctuation split off.
   The recorded 290 is almost certainly a different denominator (chapter pairs rather than distinct
   n-grams, or every chapter rather than the earlier ones). **Neither number is a scene-duplication
   signal** — both scene scans are zero — so this is a methodology difference and not a change in the
   prose. Do not cite either figure as a fact about the manuscript without re-running it.

**The gate that is a claim to be tested, and was.** The block above reports exact-duplicate paragraphs at
6+ words as **0** for the batch and its neighbours. That holds: there is no duplicated paragraph of six
words or more anywhere in 236–250. Book-wide there are 36, none of them in 241–250. **But the floor is
doing the work, and a later writer should know it.** Two exact repeats exist inside the batch below that
floor — `"Eat," he said.` at **238, 242 and 245**, and `"It stands," he said.` at **240 and 246**. They
are two- and four-word dialogue tags and they are invisible to a 6-word gate. Read for scenes, not for
paragraphs, before believing a clean duplicate count.

**TWO RESIDUAL FINDINGS THE CLOSE PASS DID NOT REPAIR, because the close phase may not write chapters.**

1. **Ch 250 drifts its own dateline. `250:91` and `250:95`.** The chapter is dated the tenth of July
   (`250:3`), and `250:93` re-anchors to the tenth outright — *"Sixteen days the finding had sat when she
   asked on the ninth. Seventeen now."* But `250:91` puts him on *"the last night of July"*, which is
   twenty-one days past the chapter's own date and **past the unwritten twelfth**, and `250:95` repeats
   *"the last night"* in the same register. **Root cause is the Batch 0005 review's finding 2, which
   removed the meta string `on the last night of the volume` by replacing `volume` with `July`.** The
   repair was correct about the meta and wrong about the date: the closing montage of Volume 5 now
   happens on the thirty-first, where the volume's hook says the twelfth is two days out and unanswered.
   This is the class `outline/volume-05.md` §9 names — a figure correctly derived on one date and carried
   to another — except that here it moves the chapter's *ending* and not a count. **`Volume 6, Batch
   0001` inherits this as its first repair.** Every other chapter in 241–250 is a strict single day and
   250 is the only one that sweeps.
2. **Provenance, not a defect.** `outline/volume-05.md` §6 movement 5 says *"Ch 246 is fixed by
   `outline/series.md`: INES DUARTE LEAVES."* **`outline/series.md` does not fix Ch 246 and does not fix
   the mechanism.** What it fixes is that Ines leaves **in Volume 5** (series.md line 41, inside *He
   hoards*, with the reason) and that Volume 5 is *"a woman deciding whether she can be with a man she
   has to audit"* (line 104). The chapter number, the filed true report and the walkout of a flat she is
   still in are Volume 5's own decisions, recorded in this block and in `state/current.md`. Nothing in
   the chapter is wrong; the outline over-claims one level of authority. **Volume 6 must not inherit the
   belief that `outline/series.md` pins Chapter 246.**

**Also recorded, no action.** `reviews/volume-05/batch-0005.md` gives 241 as 2,732 words in its
"Defects the repair did not remove" section and 2,730 in its gate block. The disk is **2,730**, which is
what the gate block and `outline/volume-05.md` §6 both say. The review file is the writer's record of
Batch 0005 and is not edited by a later phase.

**The volume's five fixed promises, verified against the chapters and not against a summary.** Ch 241 is
Agnete Lindqvist's scene — second reading room at Cobb Street, one true sentence given by name and
voluntarily, nothing compelled, **the certificate in the next house on the kitchen table and not in the
room** (`241:49`, `241:145`), and its ground, hand, name-exchange and aftermath are fresh against 205's
floor laid with record, 214's collecting room and 228's night wall. 241 does not merely avoid 214, it
negates it on the page: *"No bolt showed on the door. No pegs stood on the wall. No coat hung anywhere."*
Ch 246 is Ines leaving, and she has resolved nothing in 231–240 — the only thing done to her there is an
extract that took her off the wrong paper — and nobody asks her before it (`237:41`, `239:67`, `240:29`,
`241:25`, `242:49`, `243:85`, `244:53`, `245:37`). Ch 249 is the fifth scene at Lantern Row, gap eight.
The first of July and the fourth of July are both on the page with every named part. All four
irreversible decisions are irreversible. The volume's promise is **answered, not restated**: she was kept
off his file for the whole of it and she is the one who wanted off her own.

**Hand-off created: `workspace/volume-06/batch-0001/PROMPT.md`, chapters 251–260. It carries the 250
dateline defect above as its first repair, inherits all of `outline/volume-05.md` §7 unchanged because
**there is no `outline/volume-06.md`**, and repeats the whole gate list with the two failures Batch 0004
and Batch 0005 got wrong written into it as standing instructions.**

**THE REPAIR IS TWO LINES, NOT ONE, AND THE HAND-OFF NOW SAYS SO.** An independent review of this close
pass (`logs/close.review.log`) confirmed the defect and found the hand-off's own §10 under-stating it as
*"one phrase."* **The error is printed twice in two forms: `:91` carries the whole phrase `on the last
night of July` and `:95` carries the bare `on the last night` in the same register.** A writer reading §10
alone could license one edit and leave the chapter sweeping. Corrected in three places: §2's heading now
reads **TWO LINES**, §2 names both lines and both forms and marks `:93`'s day count as the one thing to
keep, and §10 carries the same. **`:97` is clean — *after eleven* works on any night — and Batch 0001 must
change no other sentence.**

**ACCEPTED RISK, NAMED SO IT CANNOT BE LOST.** Ordering the first Volume 6 batch to mutate a closed
volume's chapter is outside a batch's own scope by `AGENTS.md`, is carved out explicitly in the hand-off,
and was the only available path because the close phase may not write chapters. **The consequence is that
Volume 5's closure is not final until Chapter 251 runs.** If Volume 6 Batch 0001 is deferred or blocked,
a known defect sits in a closed volume indefinitely with nobody holding it. **The live gate block for that
batch, and the Volume 5 Close head block in `state/current.md`, are where the responsibility is recorded.**

**`state/current.md` IS NOW BROUGHT CURRENT.** This review also found it stale: its `Last written` block
still read *"The next phase is the volume close, and its prompt is at `workspace/volume-05/close/PROMPT.md`"*
after the close had run and produced the Batch 0001 hand-off. `PHASE_SYSTEM.md` §"The volume close"
defines the close record as writing the resolution into the state files **and** creating the next planning
phase, and only the second half had run — every batch prompt tells its writer to read `state/current.md`
first, so Batch 0001 would have opened on a record claiming the book was waiting on a finished phase.
**Corrected on the review-fix pass: the index and `Last written` now name Volume 6 Batch 0001, and a
Volume 5 Close head record carries the position, the irreversible decision, the delegated repair and the
risk above.** Batch 0005's own head block is retained below it, superseded only as to position and not as
to prose. No chapter was written, restarted, or altered by that pass.
